<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every sequence of generalized-canonical flips on compact Kähler fourfolds with rational boundary and fixed rational analytically nef b-divisor terminates when the small morphisms are projective. We prove this for normal globally Weil ℚ-factorial models with boundary coefficients less than one, allowing exceptional log discrepancies equal to one. No scaling rule or pseudo-effectivity assumption is required. The theorem applies to existing projective small diagrams with the specified opposite ample signs.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-termination)): Let $X_0$ be a normal irreducible globally Weil $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold, and let $B_0\geq0$ be a rational divisor with coefficients in $[0,1)$. Fix compatible actual canonical divisors on the birational models. Let $\mathbf M$ be the rational b-divisor represented by an analytically nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $M'$ on a projective birational morphism $X'\to X_0$ of normal compact Kähler spaces. Assume $$\begin{aligned} a(E;X_0,B_0+\mathbf M)&>0&&\text{for every prime over $X_0$},\\ a(E;X_0,B_0+\mathbf M)&\geq1&&\text{for every exceptional prime}. \end{aligned}$$ Consider a sequence of normal globally Weil $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds $$X_0\dashrightarrow X_1\dashrightarrow X_2\dashrightarrow\cdots .$$ On $X_i$, let $B_i$ be the strict transform of $B_0$, let $M_i$ be the trace of this same $\mathbf M$, and suppose $D_i=K_{X_i}+B_i+M_i$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Suppose that every step is given by a diagram $$\label{eq:step-diagram} X_i\xrightarrow{\ f_i\ }Z_i \xleftarrow{\ f_i^+\ }X_{i+1}$$ of projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibres, where $Z_i$ is normal compact Kähler, both morphisms are nonisomorphisms, $-D_i$ is $f_i$-ample, and $D_{i+1}$ is $f_i^+$-ample. Then the sequence is finite.
  - Explanation: Every given sequence terminates under the full rational generalized-canonical compact Kähler fourfold hypotheses, with fixed nef data and projective small diagrams of opposite ample signs. The result does not supply the next contraction.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-common-models)): Any finite portion of the sequence in Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a> has a smooth common model dominating its members and $X'$, projective over each member. It may also dominate any prescribed projective modification of one of those members. For a chosen member $T$, it can be arranged that the exceptional locus over $T$ is divisorial and that its union with the strict transform of $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}B_T$ has simple normal crossings. The model carries $\mathbf M$ as the pullback of $M'$.
  - Explanation: A finite portion admits a smooth common carrier projective over each member, also dominating prescribed projective modifications and arranging a simple normal crossing exceptional boundary.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-negativity)): Let $r:V\to T$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism of normal complex spaces and let $F$ be a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $V$. If $-F$ has nonnegative degree on every contracted curve and $r_*F$ is effective, then $F$ is effective.
  - Explanation: For a projective bimeromorphic morphism, effectivity follows from both nonnegative contracted-curve degrees of the negative divisor and effectivity of its pushforward.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-terminal-smooth)): A normal complex fourfold with a canonical rational line bundle and ordinary terminal singularities is smooth in codimension two.
  - Explanation: Ordinary terminality and the canonical rational line-bundle condition exclude a codimension-two singular locus on a normal complex fourfold.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-comparison)): Let $$T\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}Z \overset{g}{\longleftarrow}T^+$$ be a step satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a> between normal compact Kähler fourfolds. Write $$D_T=K_T+B_T+M_T,\qquad D_{T^+}=K_{T^+}+B_{T^+}+M_{T^+},$$ where the nef traces come from the same fixed b-divisor $\mathbf M$. Then the following assertions hold.* 1. *The exceptional images coincide: $$A:=f(\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}f)=g(\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}g).$$ With reduced structures, put $$L=f^{-1}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}f,\qquad L^+=g^{-1}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}g.$$ These are closed analytic subsets, and $$T\setminus L\simeq T^+\setminus L^+, \qquad \dim L,\dim L^+\leq2,\qquad \dim A\leq1.$$* 2. *For every divisorial place $E$ over the two models, $$\label{eq:discrepancy-monotonicity} a(E;T^+,B_{T^+}+\mathbf M) \geq a(E;T,B_T+\mathbf M).$$ The inequality is strict if the centre of $E$ on $T$ is contained in $L$, or if its centre on $T^+$ is contained in $L^+$. A place is exceptional over $T$ if and only if it is exceptional over $T^+$. In particular, the generalized canonical condition of Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a> is preserved. If it holds on $T$, then every exceptional place with centre contained in $L^+$ satisfies $$\label{eq:target-strict-discrepancy} a(E;T^+,B_{T^+}+\mathbf M)>1.$$* 3. *The exceptional loci satisfy $$\label{eq:exceptional-dimensions} \dim L+\dim L^+\geq3.$$ Consequently, if $L^+$ contains no surface, then $L$ contains a surface.
  - Explanation: The same fixed nef b-divisor and opposite ample signs yield coincident exceptional images, weak discrepancy increase everywhere, strict increase for centres contained in either exceptional locus, and the stated dimension bounds.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-nef-defect)): For every smooth model $r\colon W\to T$ that is projective over $T$ and carries the fixed nef data, the divisor $J_W$ in <a href="#eq:boundary-and-defect" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:boundary-and-defect">[eq:boundary-and-defect]</a> is an effective $r$-exceptional $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor.
  - Explanation: On a smooth projective carrier of the fixed nef data, the actual nef-trace defect is effective and exceptional.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-jacobian)): Let $W$ be a smooth complex space of dimension $n$, and let $E$ be a prime divisor on a smooth proper birational model of an open subset of $W$. Suppose its centre $C$ has codimension $c$. At a general smooth point of $C$, choose coordinates $x_1,\ldots,x_n$ with $C=(x_1=\cdots=x_c=0)$. Writing $v=\operatorname{ord}_E$, one has $$a(E;W,0)\geq\sum_{j=1}^{c}v(x_j)\geq c. \label{eq:jacobian-bound}$$ If $C$ is contained in exactly one positive component $H$ of a rational divisor $\Delta$, and $H$ is smooth at the general point of $C$ with coefficient $d\in(0,1)$, choose the coordinates so that $H=(x_1=0)$. Then $$a(E;W,\Delta) \geq (1-d)v(x_1)+\sum_{j=2}^{c}v(x_j) \geq c-d. \label{eq:one-positive-bound}$$ If $C$ is contained in no positive component of $\Delta$, then $a(E;W,\Delta)\geq c$.
  - Explanation: Jacobian orders bound discrepancies from below by the centre codimension; with exactly one smooth positive component, its coefficient gives the specified correction.
- **C8** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-low-places)): Let $(T,B_T+\mathbf M)$ be any model of a sequence as in Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a>. There is a finite set $\mathcal F_T$ of exceptional places over $T$ with the following property. If $E$ is exceptional over $T$, $$a(E;T,B_T+\mathbf M)<2, \qquad E\notin\mathcal F_T,$$ then its centre $S$ on $T$ is a surface contained in a unique positive component $H$ of $B_T$. The space $T$ and the reduced boundary support are smooth at the general point of $S$. If the coefficient of $H$ is $d$, then $$a(E;T,B_T+\mathbf M)\geq2-d. \label{eq:low-place-threshold}$$ If $B_T=0$, there are only finitely many exceptional places with log discrepancy below two.
  - Explanation: Outside a finite model-dependent set, exceptional places below the discrepancy threshold have smooth surface centres in a unique positive boundary component and satisfy its coefficient-dependent lower bound.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-ordinary-terminal)): Let $T\dashrightarrow T^+$ be a step in the sequence of Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a>, and let $S\subset L^+$ be an irreducible flipped surface. There is an analytic open subset $U\subset T^+$ meeting $S$ on which the underlying space has ordinary terminal singularities. Consequently $T^+$ is smooth at a general point of $S$.
  - Explanation: Strict target discrepancy increase together with effective boundary and nef defect gives ordinary terminality on an open set meeting each flipped surface and therefore generic smoothness there.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-witnesses)): Consider the $i$-th step of a sequence as in Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a>, with target $T^+=X_{i+1}$. Let $S$ be an irreducible surface contained in its flipped locus. There is a global prime place $E_S$, with centre $S$ on $T^+$, obtained by blowing up $S$ at its general smooth point. It is exceptional over every model of the sequence. Write $$B_{i+1}=\sum_{\ell} b_{\ell}H_{\ell}, \qquad b_{\ell}>0,$$ and let $r\colon W\to T^+$ be a smooth model that is projective over $T^+$ and carries both $E_S$ and the fixed nef data. With $J_W=r^*M_{i+1}-M_W$, one has $$a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\mathbf M) =2-\sum_{\ell}b_{\ell}\operatorname{mult}_S(H_{\ell}) -\operatorname{coeff}_{E_S}(J_W). \label{eq:witness-discrepancy}$$ In particular, $$a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\mathbf M) \in\Lambda_m:=(1,2]\cap\tfrac1m\mathbb Z =\left\{1+\tfrac1m,1+\tfrac2m,\ldots,2\right\}. \label{eq:witness-grid}$$ Its discrepancy on the source is strictly below two. If $S$ is contained in a component of $B_{i+1}$ of coefficient $b>0$, then more precisely $$a(E_S;X_i,B_i+\mathbf M) < a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\mathbf M)\leq2-b. \label{eq:witness-threshold}$$
  - Explanation: Blowing up the generically smooth flipped surface produces a global exceptional place; its target discrepancy belongs to a fixed rational grid, with the stronger boundary-dependent threshold when applicable.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-use)): Let $I$ be any set of step indices in a sequence as in Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a>. For every $i\in I$, choose a flipped surface of the $i$-th step and a witness $E_i$ as in Proposition <a href="#prop:witnesses" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:witnesses">11</a>. Then each fixed place $E$ occurs among the $E_i$ at most $m$ times. Consequently, if all the $E_i$ belong to a fixed finite collection $\mathcal F$, then $\#I\leq m\,\#\mathcal F$.
  - Explanation: Successive uses of the same place have strictly increasing target values in the fixed witness grid, bounding repetitions and the total steps witnessed by a fixed finite collection.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-cycle-loss)): Let $P$ and $Q$ be compact reduced complex analytic spaces, and let $L_P\subset P$ and $L_Q\subset Q$ be closed analytic subsets with dense complements. Suppose an isomorphism $$U=P\setminus L_P\simeq Q\setminus L_Q$$ is represented by a proper bimeromorphic graph: there is a compact reduced analytic space $G$ with proper projections $p:G\to P$ and $q:G\to Q$ such that the common open $$\label{eq:cycle-graph-open} p^{-1}(P\setminus L_P)=q^{-1}(Q\setminus L_Q)$$ is dense in $G$ and maps isomorphically to both displayed complements. If $\dim L_Q\leq1$, there is a surjection $$\mathcal C_2(P)\longrightarrow\mathcal C_2(Q), \qquad\text{and thus}\qquad c_2(P)\geq c_2(Q).$$ If $L_P$ contains an irreducible surface whose class in $H_4(P,\mathbb R)$ is nonzero, then $c_2(P)>c_2(Q)$. In particular, strict inequality holds whenever $L_P$ contains a surface and $P$ is a closed analytic subspace of a compact Kähler space.
  - Explanation: A proper graph identifying dense opens induces a surjection on surface-class spans when the target removed locus has the stated dimension bound. A nonzero removed source surface class makes rank loss strict; Kähler positivity supplies nonvanishing.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-persistence)): Let $T\dashrightarrow T^+$ be a step of a sequence as in Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a>, and let $E$ be an exceptional place whose centre on $T$ is a surface $S$. If $S$ is not contained in the source exceptional locus, its target centre is the strict transform of $S$, a surface meeting the common isomorphic open. If $S$ lies in a boundary component, its transform lies in the corresponding component.
  - Explanation: A surface centre not contained in the source exceptional locus persists through the common open and remains in its corresponding boundary component when relevant.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-coefficient-witness-count)): Consider a tail beginning with $X_N$ of a sequence as in Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a>. Let $b$ be either zero or a positive boundary coefficient. Suppose no flipping surface at any step of the tail lies in a boundary component of coefficient greater than $b$. If $b>0$, only finitely many steps of the tail have a flipped surface contained in a coefficient-$b$ boundary component. If $b=0$, only finitely many steps of the tail have any flipped surface.
  - Explanation: On a tail excluding flipping surfaces in higher-coefficient boundary components, only finitely many steps have the specified flipped surfaces; the zero-coefficient case treats arbitrary flipped surfaces.

      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $X_0$ be a normal irreducible globally Weil $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold, and let $B_0\\geq0$ be a rational divisor with coefficients in $[0,1)$. Fix compatible actual canonical divisors on the birational models. Let $\\mathbf M$ be the rational b-divisor represented by an analytically nef $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $M'$ on a projective birational morphism $X'\\to X_0$ of normal compact Kähler spaces. Assume $$\\begin{aligned}\n a(E;X_0,B_0+\\mathbf M)&>0&&\\text{for every prime over $X_0$},\\\\\n a(E;X_0,B_0+\\mathbf M)&\\geq1&&\\text{for every exceptional prime}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Consider a sequence of normal globally Weil $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds $$X_0\\dashrightarrow X_1\\dashrightarrow X_2\\dashrightarrow\\cdots .$$ On $X_i$, let $B_i$ be the strict transform of $B_0$, let $M_i$ be the trace of this same $\\mathbf M$, and suppose $D_i=K_{X_i}+B_i+M_i$ is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Suppose that every step is given by a diagram $$\\label{eq:step-diagram}\n X_i\\xrightarrow{\\ f_i\\ }Z_i\n       \\xleftarrow{\\ f_i^+\\ }X_{i+1}$$ of projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibres, where $Z_i$ is normal compact Kähler, both morphisms are nonisomorphisms, $-D_i$ is $f_i$-ample, and $D_{i+1}$ is $f_i^+$-ample. Then the sequence is finite.",
      "explanation": "Every given sequence terminates under the full rational generalized-canonical compact Kähler fourfold hypotheses, with fixed nef data and projective small diagrams of opposite ample signs. The result does not supply the next contraction.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        {

          "exact_quote": "Let $X_0$ be a normal irreducible globally Weil $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold, and let $B_0\\geq0$ be a rational divisor with coefficients in $[0,1)$. Let $\\mathbf M$ be the rational b-divisor represented by an analytically nef $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $M'$ on a projective birational morphism $X'\\to X_0$ of normal compact Kähler spaces. Assume $$\\begin{aligned}\n a(E;X_0,B_0+\\mathbf M)&>0&&\\text{for every prime over $X_0$},\\\\\n a(E;X_0,B_0+\\mathbf M)&\\geq1&&\\text{for every exceptional prime}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Consider a sequence of normal globally Weil $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds $$X_0\\dashrightarrow X_1\\dashrightarrow X_2\\dashrightarrow\\cdots .$$ On $X_i$, let $B_i$ be the strict transform of $B_0$, let $M_i$ be the trace of this same $\\mathbf M$, and suppose $D_i=K_{X_i}+B_i+M_i$ is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Suppose that every step is given by a diagram $$\\label{eq:step-diagram}\n X_i\\xrightarrow{\\ f_i\\ }Z_i\n       \\xleftarrow{\\ f_i^+\\ }X_{i+1}$$ of projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibres, where $Z_i$ is normal compact Kähler, both morphisms are nonisomorphisms, $-D_i$ is $f_i$-ample, and $D_{i+1}$ is $f_i^+$-ample. Then the sequence is finite.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.2"
        }

Assume $$\\begin{aligned}\n a(E;X_0,B_0+\\mathbf M)&>0&&\\text{for every prime over $X_0$},\\\\\n a(E;X_0,B_0+\\mathbf M)&\\geq1&&\\text{for every exceptional prime}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Consider a sequence of normal globally Weil $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds $$X_0\\dashrightarrow X_1\\dashrightarrow X_2\\dashrightarrow\\cdots .$$ On $X_i$, let $B_i$ be the strict transform of $B_0$, let $M_i$ be the trace of this same $\\mathbf M$, and suppose $D_i=K_{X_i}+B_i+M_i$ is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Then the sequence is finite.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-common-models",
        "exact_quote": "Any finite portion of the sequence in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a> has a smooth common model dominating its members and $X'$, projective over each member. It may also dominate any prescribed projective modification of one of those members. For a chosen member $T$, it can be arranged that the exceptional locus over $T$ is divisorial and that its union with the strict transform of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}B_T$ has simple normal crossings. The model carries $\\mathbf M$ as the pullback of $M'$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-negativity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r:V\\to T$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism of normal complex spaces and let $F$ be a $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $V$. If $-F$ has nonnegative degree on every contracted curve and $r_*F$ is effective, then $F$ is effective.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-negativity",

          "source_id": "PM-056.2"
        }
      ],

      "explanation": "For a projective bimeromorphic morphism, effectivity follows from both nonnegative contracted-curve degrees of the negative divisor and effectivity of its pushforward.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$T\\overset{f}{\\longrightarrow}Z\n   \\overset{g}{\\longleftarrow}T^+$$ be a step satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a> between normal compact Kähler fourfolds. Write $$D_T=K_T+B_T+M_T,\\qquad\n D_{T^+}=K_{T^+}+B_{T^+}+M_{T^+},$$ where the nef traces come from the same fixed b-divisor $\\mathbf M$. Then the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *The exceptional images coincide: $$A:=f(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Exc}}f)=g(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Exc}}g).$$ With reduced structures, put $$L=f^{-1}(A)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Exc}}f,\\qquad\n     L^+=g^{-1}(A)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Exc}}g.$$ These are closed analytic subsets, and $$T\\setminus L\\simeq T^+\\setminus L^+,\n     \\qquad \\dim L,\\dim L^+\\leq2,\\qquad \\dim A\\leq1.$$*\n\n\n\n2.  *For every divisorial place $E$ over the two models, $$\\label{eq:discrepancy-monotonicity}\n     a(E;T^+,B_{T^+}+\\mathbf M)\n     \\geq a(E;T,B_T+\\mathbf M).$$ The inequality is strict if the centre of $E$ on $T$ is contained in $L$, or if its centre on $T^+$ is contained in $L^+$. A place is exceptional over $T$ if and only if it is exceptional over $T^+$. In particular, the generalized canonical condition of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a> is preserved. If it holds on $T$, then every exceptional place with centre contained in $L^+$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:target-strict-discrepancy}\n     a(E;T^+,B_{T^+}+\\mathbf M)>1.$$*\n\n\n3.  *The exceptional loci satisfy $$\\label{eq:exceptional-dimensions}\n     \\dim L+\\dim L^+\\geq3.$$ Consequently, if $L^+$ contains no surface, then $L$ contains a surface.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-comparison",

In particular, the generalized canonical condition of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a> is preserved. If it holds on $T$, then every exceptional place with centre contained in $L^+$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:target-strict-discrepancy}\n     a(E;T^+,B_{T^+}+\\mathbf M)>1.$$*\n\n\n3.  *The exceptional loci satisfy $$\\label{eq:exceptional-dimensions}\n     \\dim L+\\dim L^+\\geq3.$$ Consequently, if $L^+$ contains no surface, then $L$ contains a surface.",

        "exact_quote": "For every smooth model $r\\colon W\\to T$ that is projective over $T$ and carries the fixed nef data, the divisor $J_W$ in <a href=\"#eq:boundary-and-defect\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boundary-and-defect\">[eq:boundary-and-defect]</a> is an effective $r$-exceptional $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-jacobian",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a smooth complex space of dimension $n$, and let $E$ be a prime divisor on a smooth proper birational model of an open subset of $W$. Suppose its centre $C$ has codimension $c$. At a general smooth point of $C$, choose coordinates $x_1,\\ldots,x_n$ with $C=(x_1=\\cdots=x_c=0)$. Writing $v=\\operatorname{ord}_E$, one has $$a(E;W,0)\\geq\\sum_{j=1}^{c}v(x_j)\\geq c.\n \\label{eq:jacobian-bound}$$ If $C$ is contained in exactly one positive component $H$ of a rational divisor $\\Delta$, and $H$ is smooth at the general point of $C$ with coefficient $d\\in(0,1)$, choose the coordinates so that $H=(x_1=0)$. Then $$a(E;W,\\Delta)\n \\geq (1-d)v(x_1)+\\sum_{j=2}^{c}v(x_j)\n \\geq c-d.\n \\label{eq:one-positive-bound}$$ If $C$ is contained in no positive component of $\\Delta$, then $a(E;W,\\Delta)\\geq c$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-jacobian",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a smooth complex space of dimension $n$, and let $E$ be a prime divisor on a smooth proper birational model of an open subset of $W$. Suppose its centre $C$ has codimension $c$. At a general smooth point of $C$, choose coordinates $x_1,\\ldots,x_n$ with $C=(x_1=\\cdots=x_c=0)$. Writing $v=\\operatorname{ord}_E$, one has $$a(E;W,0)\\geq\\sum_{j=1}^{c}v(x_j)\\geq c.\n \\label{eq:jacobian-bound}$$ If $C$ is contained in exactly one positive component $H$ of a rational divisor $\\Delta$, and $H$ is smooth at the general point of $C$ with coefficient $d\\in(0,1)$, choose the coordinates so that $H=(x_1=0)$. Then $$a(E;W,\\Delta)\n \\geq (1-d)v(x_1)+\\sum_{j=2}^{c}v(x_j)\n \\geq c-d.\n \\label{eq:one-positive-bound}$$ If $C$ is contained in no positive component of $\\Delta$, then $a(E;W,\\Delta)\\geq c$.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.2"
        }
      ],

Then $$a(E;W,\\Delta)\n \\geq (1-d)v(x_1)+\\sum_{j=2}^{c}v(x_j)\n \\geq c-d.\n \\label{eq:one-positive-bound}$$ If $C$ is contained in no positive component of $\\Delta$, then $a(E;W,\\Delta)\\geq c$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-low-places",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T,B_T+\\mathbf M)$ be any model of a sequence as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a>. There is a finite set $\\mathcal F_T$ of exceptional places over $T$ with the following property. If $E$ is exceptional over $T$, $$a(E;T,B_T+\\mathbf M)<2,\n \\qquad E\\notin\\mathcal F_T,$$ then its centre $S$ on $T$ is a surface contained in a unique positive component $H$ of $B_T$. The space $T$ and the reduced boundary support are smooth at the general point of $S$. If the coefficient of $H$ is $d$, then $$a(E;T,B_T+\\mathbf M)\\geq2-d.\n \\label{eq:low-place-threshold}$$ If $B_T=0$, there are only finitely many exceptional places with log discrepancy below two.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-low-places",

The space $T$ and the reduced boundary support are smooth at the general point of $S$. If the coefficient of $H$ is $d$, then $$a(E;T,B_T+\\mathbf M)\\geq2-d.\n \\label{eq:low-place-threshold}$$ If $B_T=0$, there are only finitely many exceptional places with log discrepancy below two.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-ordinary-terminal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T\\dashrightarrow T^+$ be a step in the sequence of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a>, and let $S\\subset L^+$ be an irreducible flipped surface.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-witnesses",
        "exact_quote": "Consider the $i$-th step of a sequence as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a>, with target $T^+=X_{i+1}$. Let $S$ be an irreducible surface contained in its flipped locus. There is a global prime place $E_S$, with centre $S$ on $T^+$, obtained by blowing up $S$ at its general smooth point. It is exceptional over every model of the sequence. Write $$B_{i+1}=\\sum_{\\ell} b_{\\ell}H_{\\ell},\n \\qquad b_{\\ell}>0,$$ and let $r\\colon W\\to T^+$ be a smooth model that is projective over $T^+$ and carries both $E_S$ and the fixed nef data. With $J_W=r^*M_{i+1}-M_W$, one has $$a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\n =2-\\sum_{\\ell}b_{\\ell}\\operatorname{mult}_S(H_{\\ell})\n       -\\operatorname{coeff}_{E_S}(J_W).\n \\label{eq:witness-discrepancy}$$ In particular, $$a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\n \\in\\Lambda_m:=(1,2]\\cap\\tfrac1m\\mathbb Z\n =\\left\\{1+\\tfrac1m,1+\\tfrac2m,\\ldots,2\\right\\}.\n \\label{eq:witness-grid}$$ Its discrepancy on the source is strictly below two. If $S$ is contained in a component of $B_{i+1}$ of coefficient $b>0$, then more precisely $$a(E_S;X_i,B_i+\\mathbf M)\n < a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\\leq2-b.\n \\label{eq:witness-threshold}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-056.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-witnesses",
          "exact_quote": "Consider the $i$-th step of a sequence as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a>, with target $T^+=X_{i+1}$. With $J_W=r^*M_{i+1}-M_W$, one has $$a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\n =2-\\sum_{\\ell}b_{\\ell}\\operatorname{mult}_S(H_{\\ell})\n       -\\operatorname{coeff}_{E_S}(J_W).\n \\label{eq:witness-discrepancy}$$ In particular, $$a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\n \\in\\Lambda_m:=(1,2]\\cap\\tfrac1m\\mathbb Z\n =\\left\\{1+\\tfrac1m,1+\\tfrac2m,\\ldots,2\\right\\}.\n \\label{eq:witness-grid}$$ Its discrepancy on the source is strictly below two.           "source_id": "PM-056.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-use",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I$ be any set of step indices in a sequence as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a>. For every $i\\in I$, choose a flipped surface of the $i$-th step and a witness $E_i$ as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:witnesses\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:witnesses\">11</a>. Then each fixed place $E$ occurs among the $E_i$ at most $m$ times. Consequently, if all the $E_i$ belong to a fixed finite collection $\\mathcal F$, then $\\#I\\leq m\\,\\#\\mathcal F$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cycle-loss",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ and $Q$ be compact reduced complex analytic spaces, and let $L_P\\subset P$ and $L_Q\\subset Q$ be closed analytic subsets with dense complements. Suppose an isomorphism $$U=P\\setminus L_P\\simeq Q\\setminus L_Q$$ is represented by a proper bimeromorphic graph: there is a compact reduced analytic space $G$ with proper projections $p:G\\to P$ and $q:G\\to Q$ such that the common open $$\\label{eq:cycle-graph-open}\n p^{-1}(P\\setminus L_P)=q^{-1}(Q\\setminus L_Q)$$ is dense in $G$ and maps isomorphically to both displayed complements. If $\\dim L_Q\\leq1$, there is a surjection $$\\mathcal C_2(P)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal C_2(Q),\n \\qquad\\text{and thus}\\qquad c_2(P)\\geq c_2(Q).$$ If $L_P$ contains an irreducible surface whose class in $H_4(P,\\mathbb R)$ is nonzero, then $c_2(P)>c_2(Q)$. In particular, strict inequality holds whenever $L_P$ contains a surface and $P$ is a closed analytic subspace of a compact Kähler space.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cycle-loss",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P$ and $Q$ be compact reduced complex analytic spaces, and let $L_P\\subset P$ and $L_Q\\subset Q$ be closed analytic subsets with dense complements. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Analytically nef class** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A class in the real Bott–Chern space $H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(X,\mathbb R)$ is *analytically nef* if it lies in the closure of the Kähler cone.
- **Globally Weil rationally factorial space** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We call a normal compact space *globally Weil $\mathbb Q$-factorial* if every prime Weil divisor defined on the whole space is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and some positive reflexive power of its canonical sheaf is a line bundle.
- **Fixed rational b-divisor** ([source](#pm-h-3)): These data define a rational b-divisor $\mathbf M$: on a model dominating $X'$ its trace is the pullback of $M'$, and on another model its trace is the pushforward from a common higher model.
- **Generalized discrepancy boundary** ([source](#eq:structure)): On a smooth model $p:W\to X$ dominating $X'$, define the actual rational divisor $\Delta_W$ by $$\label{eq:structure} K_W+\Delta_W+M_W=p^*(K_X+B+M_X).$$
- **Generalized log discrepancy** ([source](#eq:structure)): For a prime divisor $E$ on $W$, its *generalized log discrepancy* is $$a(E;X,B+\mathbf M)=1-\operatorname{coeff}_E\Delta_W.$$
- **Divisorial place** ([source](#eq:structure)): Here a *prime over $X$*, or a *divisorial place*, is a prime divisor on a proper bimeromorphic model, identified with its strict transforms on common higher models.
- **Centre and exceptional place** ([source](#eq:structure)): Its centre is its reduced irreducible image, and it is *exceptional* if this image has codimension at least two.
- **Generalized klt and canonical pair** ([source](#eq:structure)): The pair is *generalized klt* if all these discrepancies are positive, and *generalized canonical* if the discrepancies of exceptional primes are at least one.
- **Projective morphism** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We use *projective* for a proper morphism admitting a relatively ample holomorphic line bundle.
- **Relatively ample rational divisor** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor is relatively ample if a positive Cartier multiple is so.
- **Small morphism** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A proper bimeromorphic morphism is *small* if its exceptional locus contains no prime divisor.
- **Flipping and flipped surface** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Surfaces contained in the source and target exceptional loci are called *flipping* and *flipped surfaces*, respectively.
- **Surface-cycle span and rank** ([source](#eq:cycle-rank)): For a compact reduced complex analytic space $V$, define $$\label{eq:cycle-rank} \begin{split} \mathcal C_2(V) &:=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb R}\{[S]\in H_4(V,\mathbb R): S\subset V\text{ is an irreducible compact analytic surface}\},\\ c_2(V)&:=\dim_{\mathbb R}\mathcal C_2(V). \end{split}$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Initial generalized log discrepancy | > | $0$ | Every prime over the initial model. |
| Q2 | Initial exceptional generalized log discrepancy | ≥ | $1$ | Every exceptional prime over the initial model. |
| Q3 | Exceptional locus dimension | ≤ | $2$ | Each source and target exceptional locus. |
| Q4 | Common exceptional image dimension | ≤ | $1$ | The common exceptional image on the contraction base. |
| Q5 | Sum of exceptional locus dimensions | ≥ | $3$ | Source and target exceptional loci. |
| Q6 | Target discrepancy of exceptional places centred in the flipped locus | > | $1$ | Exceptional places whose centres are contained in the target exceptional locus. |
| Q7 | Local zero-boundary log discrepancy | ≥ | $c$ | Centre codimension and positive integral coordinate orders. |
| Q8 | Local discrepancy with one positive boundary component | ≥ | $c-d$ | The positive coefficient lies in the stated open interval. |
| Q9 | Discrepancy outside the finite low-place exceptional set | ≥ | $2-d$ | Exceptional places below two outside the finite exceptional set; unique positive coefficient d. |
| Q10 | Successive blowup-chain discrepancy | = | $1+j(1-d)$ | Positive chain index and coefficient d strictly between zero and one. |
| Q11 | Blowup-chain cutoff index | = | $\left\lceil\frac{d}{1-d}\right\rceil$ | Rational positive boundary coefficient below one. |
| Q12 | Target discrepancy of a witness | ≤ | $2$ | A global witness obtained by blowing up the surface. |
| Q13 | Target witness discrepancy in a boundary component | ≤ | $2-b$ | The coefficient b of the containing component. |
| Q14 | Uses of a fixed witness place | ≤ | $m$ | The fixed positive integer clearing the nef datum and boundary coefficients. |
| Q15 | Steps witnessed by a fixed finite collection | ≤ | $m\,\#\mathcal F$ | Step indices whose chosen witnesses all belong to the fixed finite collection. |
| Q16 | Target surface-cycle rank | < | $c_2(P)$ | Target removed locus of dimension at most one and a nonzero removed source surface class. |

            "exact_quote": "Let $X_0$ be a normal irreducible globally Weil $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold, and let $B_0\\geq0$ be a rational divisor with coefficients in $[0,1)$. Assume $$\\begin{aligned}\n a(E;X_0,B_0+\\mathbf M)&>0&&\\text{for every prime over $X_0$},\\\\\n a(E;X_0,B_0+\\mathbf M)&\\geq1&&\\text{for every exceptional prime}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Consider a sequence of normal globally Weil $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds $$X_0\\dashrightarrow X_1\\dashrightarrow X_2\\dashrightarrow\\cdots .$$ On $X_i$, let $B_i$ be the strict transform of $B_0$, let $M_i$ be the trace of this same $\\mathbf M$, and suppose $D_i=K_{X_i}+B_i+M_i$ is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Suppose that every step is given by a diagram $$\\label{eq:step-diagram}\n X_i\\xrightarrow{\\ f_i\\ }Z_i\n       \\xleftarrow{\\ f_i^+\\ }X_{i+1}$$ of projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibres, where $Z_i$ is normal compact Kähler, both morphisms are nonisomorphisms, $-D_i$ is $f_i$-ample, and $D_{i+1}$ is $f_i^+$-ample. Then the sequence is finite.",

      "kind": "bound",

      "regime": {

        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-lem-jacobian",
            "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a smooth complex space of dimension $n$, and let $E$ be a prime divisor on a smooth proper birational model of an open subset of $W$. Suppose its centre $C$ has codimension $c$. At a general smooth point of $C$, choose coordinates $x_1,\\ldots,x_n$ with $C=(x_1=\\cdots=x_c=0)$. Writing $v=\\operatorname{ord}_E$, one has $$a(E;W,0)\\geq\\sum_{j=1}^{c}v(x_j)\\geq c.\n \\label{eq:jacobian-bound}$$ If $C$ is contained in exactly one positive component $H$ of a rational divisor $\\Delta$, and $H$ is smooth at the general point of $C$ with coefficient $d\\in(0,1)$, choose the coordinates so that $H=(x_1=0)$. Then $$a(E;W,\\Delta)\n \\geq (1-d)v(x_1)+\\sum_{j=2}^{c}v(x_j)\n \\geq c-d.\n \\label{eq:one-positive-bound}$$ If $C$ is contained in no positive component of $\\Delta$, then $a(E;W,\\Delta)\\geq c$.",
            "source_id": "PM-056.2"
          }
        ],

      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "Discrepancy outside the finite low-place exceptional set",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "A fixed model of the generalized-canonical sequence.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-lem-low-places",
            "exact_quote": "Let $(T,B_T+\\mathbf M)$ be any model of a sequence as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a>. There is a finite set $\\mathcal F_T$ of exceptional places over $T$ with the following property. If $E$ is exceptional over $T$, $$a(E;T,B_T+\\mathbf M)<2,\n \\qquad E\\notin\\mathcal F_T,$$ then its centre $S$ on $T$ is a surface contained in a unique positive component $H$ of $B_T$. The space $T$ and the reduced boundary support are smooth at the general point of $S$. If the coefficient of $H$ is $d$, then $$a(E;T,B_T+\\mathbf M)\\geq2-d.\n \\label{eq:low-place-threshold}$$ If $B_T=0$, there are only finitely many exceptional places with log discrepancy below two.",
            "source_id": "PM-056.2"
          }
        ],
        "parameter_scope": "Exceptional places below two outside the finite exceptional set; unique positive coefficient d.",
        "quantifiers": [

            "source_id": "PM-056.2"
          }
        ]

          "exact_quote": "\\in\\Lambda_m:=(1,2]\\cap\\tfrac1m\\mathbb Z",

      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "Q12",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "Target discrepancy of a witness",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "Flipped surface with a smooth general point and fixed rational nef data.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-prop-witnesses",
            "exact_quote": "Consider the $i$-th step of a sequence as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a>, with target $T^+=X_{i+1}$. Let $S$ be an irreducible surface contained in its flipped locus. There is a global prime place $E_S$, with centre $S$ on $T^+$, obtained by blowing up $S$ at its general smooth point. It is exceptional over every model of the sequence. Write $$B_{i+1}=\\sum_{\\ell} b_{\\ell}H_{\\ell},\n \\qquad b_{\\ell}>0,$$ and let $r\\colon W\\to T^+$ be a smooth model that is projective over $T^+$ and carries both $E_S$ and the fixed nef data. With $J_W=r^*M_{i+1}-M_W$, one has $$a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\n =2-\\sum_{\\ell}b_{\\ell}\\operatorname{mult}_S(H_{\\ell})\n       -\\operatorname{coeff}_{E_S}(J_W).\n \\label{eq:witness-discrepancy}$$ In particular, $$a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\n \\in\\Lambda_m:=(1,2]\\cap\\tfrac1m\\mathbb Z\n =\\left\\{1+\\tfrac1m,1+\\tfrac2m,\\ldots,2\\right\\}.\n \\label{eq:witness-grid}$$ Its discrepancy on the source is strictly below two. If $S$ is contained in a component of $B_{i+1}$ of coefficient $b>0$, then more precisely $$a(E_S;X_i,B_i+\\mathbf M)\n < a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\\leq2-b.\n \\label{eq:witness-threshold}$$",
            "source_id": "PM-056.2"
          }
        ],
        "parameter_scope": "A global witness obtained by blowing up the surface.",
        "quantifiers": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-prop-witnesses",
            "exact_quote": "\\in\\Lambda_m:=(1,2]\\cap\\tfrac1m\\mathbb Z",
            "source_id": "PM-056.2"
          }
        ]
      },
      "relation": "≤",
      "value_latex": "2"
    },
    {
      "computational_model": null,
      "effectivity": "not_applicable",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-witnesses",
          "exact_quote": "< a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\\leq2-b.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.2"

      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "Target witness discrepancy in a boundary component",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "Flipped surface contained in a positive boundary component.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-prop-witnesses",
            "exact_quote": "Consider the $i$-th step of a sequence as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:termination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:termination\">1</a>, with target $T^+=X_{i+1}$. Let $S$ be an irreducible surface contained in its flipped locus. There is a global prime place $E_S$, with centre $S$ on $T^+$, obtained by blowing up $S$ at its general smooth point. It is exceptional over every model of the sequence. Write $$B_{i+1}=\\sum_{\\ell} b_{\\ell}H_{\\ell},\n \\qquad b_{\\ell}>0,$$ and let $r\\colon W\\to T^+$ be a smooth model that is projective over $T^+$ and carries both $E_S$ and the fixed nef data. With $J_W=r^*M_{i+1}-M_W$, one has $$a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\n =2-\\sum_{\\ell}b_{\\ell}\\operatorname{mult}_S(H_{\\ell})\n       -\\operatorname{coeff}_{E_S}(J_W).\n \\label{eq:witness-discrepancy}$$ In particular, $$a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\n \\in\\Lambda_m:=(1,2]\\cap\\tfrac1m\\mathbb Z\n =\\left\\{1+\\tfrac1m,1+\\tfrac2m,\\ldots,2\\right\\}.\n \\label{eq:witness-grid}$$ Its discrepancy on the source is strictly below two. If $S$ is contained in a component of $B_{i+1}$ of coefficient $b>0$, then more precisely $$a(E_S;X_i,B_i+\\mathbf M)\n < a(E_S;X_{i+1},B_{i+1}+\\mathbf M)\\leq2-b.\n \\label{eq:witness-threshold}$$",
            "source_id": "PM-056.2"
          }
        ],
        "parameter_scope": "The coefficient b of the containing component.",
        "quantifiers": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-prop-witnesses",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-common-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-common-models): Dominating graph components, normalization, resolution and principalization. -> Smooth common model carrying the nef data.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-negativity) / [proof](#pm-h-8): Apply analytic negativity to actual rational divisors. -> Effective actual divisor.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-comparison): Use the effective graph divisor and its full exceptional support to compare coefficients. -> Strict discrepancy increase for centres contained in either exceptional locus and the dimension inequality.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-nef-defect) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nef-defect): Apply negativity to the pulled-back trace minus the nef datum. -> Effective exceptional nef-trace defect.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-jacobian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-jacobian): Estimate the order of the pulled-back volume form in transverse coordinates. -> Codimension-dependent lower discrepancy bounds.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-low-places) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-low-places): Separate positive boundary components and follow forced repeated blowup centres. -> Finite exceptional set and classification of the remaining low places.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-smooth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-smooth): Take transverse surface slices and contract exceptional curves with positive relative canonical coefficients. -> Smoothness in codimension two.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-ordinary-terminal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ordinary-terminal): Delete images of zero ordinary relative canonical coefficients and check all local places. -> Generic smoothness along each flipped surface.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-prop-witnesses) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-witnesses): Blow up the reduced global surface ideal and compute at its smooth general point. -> Global witnesses with target discrepancies on the finite rational grid.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-use) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-use): Count strictly increasing target values in the fixed witness grid. -> Finite use of each witness and of any fixed finite witness collection.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-lem-cycle-loss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cycle-loss): Use Borel–Moore localization and Kähler positivity on surface-class spans. -> Surjective cycle-span map and strict rank loss when a source surface is removed.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-lem-persistence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-persistence): Follow the dense portion of a surface centre through the common isomorphic open. -> Persistent strict-transform surface centre.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-lem-coefficient-witness-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coefficient-witness-count): Exclude higher-coefficient centres by persistence and bound the fixed finite witnesses. -> Finitely many relevant flipped-surface steps.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-thm-termination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coefficient-witness-count-2): Descend through positive boundary coefficients, pass to tails, then use strict descent of finite nonnegative surface-cycle ranks. -> Finiteness of every given sequence satisfying the theorem hypotheses.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **chain**: The repeated blowup chain has the stated discrepancy formula and a finite cutoff below discrepancy two.
- **strict-centre**: Strict discrepancy increase requires containment of the centre in the corresponding exceptional locus.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **workflow**: Infer termination of software rewrites or agent workflows from increasing scores. -> The iteration has no established projective small flip diagram on compact Kähler fourfolds.
- **intersection**: Claim strict discrepancy increase when a centre merely meets an exceptional locus. -> Intersection does not establish the containment required for strictness.
- **finite-certificate**: Treat the finite blowup-chain arithmetic as a certificate for all geometric flips. -> Arithmetic checks do not establish the analytic and birational hypotheses or the surface-cycle argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The repeated-blowup proof gives a finite constructive rational calculation below discrepancy two. This calculation does not construct flips or certify termination of a supplied geometric sequence.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of integer pairs [p,q] encoding rational d=p/q.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1<=p<q<=64; at most 4096 pairs; no geometric objects supplied.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list [J, [[j, rational discrepancy], ...]] for discrepancy strictly below two.
# TIME: O(number of pairs times maximum q) exact arithmetic operations.
# SPACE: O(number of pairs times maximum q) output storage.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq exact rational recurrence, integer ceiling.
# FAILURE: malformed or out-of-contract input raises an error; no geometric certification.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq
pairs = json.load(sys.stdin)
assert isinstance(pairs, list) and len(pairs) <= 4096
results = []
for p, q in pairs:
    assert type(p) is int and type(q) is int and 1 <= p < q <= 64
    J = (p + (q-p) - 1) // (q-p)
    gap = fmpq(q-p, q)
    a = fmpq(1)
    eligible = []
    for j in range(1, J+2):
        a += gap
        if a < 2:
            eligible.append([j, str(a)])
    results.append([J, eligible])
print(json.dumps(results, separators=(',', ':')))
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys as system\nimport json as codec\nfrom fractions import Fraction as Rational\nraw = codec.loads(open(system.argv[1]).read())\ntruth = []\nfor numerator, denominator in raw:\n    if not (type(numerator) is int and type(denominator) is int and 1 <= numerator < denominator <= 64):\n        raise ValueError('invalid rational boundary coefficient')\n    difference = denominator - numerator\n    stop = next(k for k in range(1, denominator+1) if (k+1)*difference >= denominator)\n    terms = [[index, str(Rational(denominator + index*difference, denominator))]\n             for index in range(1, denominator+1) if index*difference < denominator]\n    truth.append([stop, terms])\nclaimed = codec.loads(system.argv[3])\nactual = codec.loads(open(system.argv[2]).read())\nif claimed != truth:\n    system.exit(4)\nif actual != truth:\n    system.exit(6)\nsystem.exit(0)\n",

    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of integer pairs [p,q] encoding rational d=p/q.\n# PRECONDITIONS: 1<=p<q<=64; at most 4096 pairs; no geometric objects supplied.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON list [J, [[j, rational discrepancy], ...]] for discrepancy strictly below two.\n# TIME: O(number of pairs times maximum q) exact arithmetic operations.\n# SPACE: O(number of pairs times maximum q) output storage.\n# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq exact rational recurrence, integer ceiling.\n# FAILURE: malformed or out-of-contract input raises an error; no geometric certification.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\npairs = json.load(sys.stdin)\nassert isinstance(pairs, list) and len(pairs) <= 4096\nresults = []\nfor p, q in pairs:\n    assert type(p) is int and type(q) is int and 1 <= p < q <= 64\n    J = (p + (q-p) - 1) // (q-p)\n    gap = fmpq(q-p, q)\n    a = fmpq(1)\n    eligible = []\n    for j in range(1, J+2):\n        a += gap\n        if a < 2:\n            eligible.append([j, str(a)])\n    results.append([J, eligible])\nprint(json.dumps(results, separators=(',', ':')))\n",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-geometric-category** (hypothesis): The termination result concerns normal irreducible globally Weil rationally factorial compact Kähler fourfolds with effective rational boundary coefficients in the stated interval. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to arbitrary state transitions or to spaces outside its geometric category.']
- **L-initial-discrepancies** (hypothesis): The initial pair must have positive generalized log discrepancies for every prime and the canonical lower bound for exceptional primes. Equality at the exceptional canonical bound is permitted. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to an arbitrary generalized klt pair without the exceptional canonical bound or excluding equality as if generalized terminality were required.']
- **L-fixed-nef** (hypothesis): The rational analytically nef data are fixed throughout the sequence, and the traces on the successive models belong to this same b-divisor. Blocked use: ['Replacing the nef data between steps or substituting curve-degree nonnegativity for the required absolute analytic nefness.']
- **L-projective-signs** (hypothesis): Every supplied step must have projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibres and the specified opposite relative ample signs. Blocked use: ['Dropping projectivity, replacing smallness by arbitrary birationality, or treating an arbitrary improvement step as a flip.']
- **L-canonical-data** (hypothesis): Compatible actual canonical divisor representatives are supplied data; their existence is not inferred from a canonical rational line bundle alone. Blocked use: ['Comparing only divisor classes or assuming that the canonical rational line bundle supplies the prescribed representatives.']
- **L-global-factorial** (edge_case): Global Weil rational factoriality does not assert factoriality of local analytic rings or rational Cartierness of every rank-one reflexive sheaf. Blocked use: ['Strengthening the global condition to local analytic factoriality.']
- **L-existence** (nonconstructive): The theorem proves finiteness of given diagrams and does not guarantee existence or selection of a desired next step. Blocked use: ['Using termination as a contraction-existence theorem or an executable procedure for choosing flips.']
- **L-no-extra-positivity** (edge_case): Pseudo-effectivity, scaling, and a relative Picard-number restriction are not hypotheses of this theorem. Blocked use: ['Adding these conditions as if the theorem required them.']
- **L-canonical-not-smooth** (edge_case): Generalized canonicity does not imply generic smoothness along an arbitrary surface; the proof establishes the required smoothness along flipped surfaces using strict discrepancy increase. Blocked use: ['Assuming ordinary terminality or smoothness of the whole model from generalized canonicity.']
- **L-strict-centres** (hypothesis): Strict discrepancy increase requires containment of the centre in an exceptional locus. Blocked use: ['Replacing containment by mere intersection with the exceptional locus.']
- **L-negativity** (hypothesis): The analytic negativity lemma requires both the contracted-curve degree condition for the negative divisor and effectivity of its pushforward. Blocked use: ['Inferring effectivity from relative nefness of the negative divisor alone.']
- **L-low-place-exclusions** (edge_case): The low-discrepancy finite exceptional set is attached to a fixed model; repeated blowups above exceptional intersections must be included. Blocked use: ['Assuming a uniform size along the sequence or counting only the first blowup.']
- **L-cartier-local** (edge_case): The clearing integer makes the nef trace integral Weil everywhere and Cartier on the smooth locus; it does not give global Cartierness of that multiple. Blocked use: ['Claiming a uniform global Cartier index for nef traces on all models.']
- **L-cycle-map** (hypothesis): Cycle loss concerns the spans of surface classes, and strict rank loss requires a nonzero removed surface class. Blocked use: ['Claiming surjectivity on all homology or strict rank loss without nonvanishing.']
- **L-discreteness** (edge_case): Only witness target discrepancies are constrained to the fixed finite grid; the argument requires no discreteness at intervening steps. Blocked use: ['Assuming every discrepancy at every step lies in the witness grid.']
- **L-finite-arithmetic-scope** (formal_scope): Finite exact arithmetic can check the displayed blowup-chain calculation, but does not instantiate the analytic spaces, divisorial places, or flip diagrams required by the termination theorem. Blocked use: ['Publishing an arithmetic harness result as a certificate of geometric termination or of generic software-loop termination.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every sequence of generalized-canonical flips on compact Kähler fourfolds with rational boundary and fixed rational analytically nef b-divisor terminates when the small morphisms are projective. We prove this for normal globally Weil ℚ-factorial models with boundary coefficients less than one, allowing exceptional log discrepancies equal to one. No scaling rule or pseudo-effectivity assumption is required. The theorem applies to existing projective small diagrams with the specified opposite ample signs.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction and the main theorem

A flip replaces a small contraction on which an adjoint divisor is negative by a small contraction on which its transform is positive. Termination asks whether this local improvement can continue indefinitely. Generalized pairs enlarge the adjoint from $K_X+B$ to $K_X+B+M_X$, where $M_X$ is the trace of nef data on a higher birational model. The nef contribution can change discrepancies even where the ordinary boundary is absent. On a compact Kähler space, one must also distinguish analytic nefness from nonnegative degrees on curves.

We prove termination in the generalized-canonical case in dimension four, for rational nef data fixed throughout the sequence. The proof does not choose the contractions. It applies to every sequence of projective small diagrams with the required opposite ample signs. Exceptional log discrepancies may equal one. The resulting canonical singularities need not be smooth in codimension two, so the argument must establish smoothness precisely along the flipped surfaces that it counts. We first specify the global analytic category of the theorem.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The data and the statement

All spaces are complex, and a fourfold is irreducible and four-dimensional. A Kähler form on a normal space is given by smooth strictly plurisubharmonic local potentials in analytic embeddings. A class in the real Bott–Chern space $H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(X,\mathbb R)$ is *analytically nef* if it lies in the closure of the Kähler cone. For a rational line bundle or a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor, this means that its first Chern class is nef in this sense.

We call a normal compact space *globally Weil $\mathbb Q$-factorial* if every prime Weil divisor defined on the whole space is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and some positive reflexive power of its canonical sheaf is a line bundle. This condition does not assert factoriality of the local analytic rings, or $\mathbb Q$-Cartierness of every rank-one reflexive sheaf. Compatible actual canonical divisor representatives on the birational models are part of the supplied data. We do not infer their existence from the canonical rational line-bundle condition alone.

Fix a projective birational morphism $$\pi:X'\longrightarrow X$$ of normal compact Kähler spaces and an analytically nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $M'$ on $X'$. These data define a rational b-divisor $\mathbf M$: on a model dominating $X'$ its trace is the pullback of $M'$, and on another model its trace is the pushforward from a common higher model. We write $M_T$ for the trace on a model $T$. The divisor $M'$ need not be effective. Replacing $X'$ by a higher model and $M'$ by its pullback leaves $\mathbf M$ unchanged.

<a id="eq:structure"></a>
Let $B\geq0$ be a rational divisor of finite support with coefficients less than one, and assume $D=K_X+B+M_X$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. On a smooth model $p:W\to X$ dominating $X'$, define the actual rational divisor $\Delta_W$ by $$\label{eq:structure}
 K_W+\Delta_W+M_W=p^*(K_X+B+M_X).$$ For a prime divisor $E$ on $W$, its *generalized log discrepancy* is $$a(E;X,B+\mathbf M)=1-\operatorname{coeff}_E\Delta_W.$$ Here a *prime over $X$*, or a *divisorial place*, is a prime divisor on a proper bimeromorphic model, identified with its strict transforms on common higher models. Its centre is its reduced irreducible image, and it is *exceptional* if this image has codimension at least two. Equation <a href="#eq:structure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:structure">[eq:structure]</a> on higher models defines its discrepancy independently of the carrying model. The pair is *generalized klt* if all these discrepancies are positive, and *generalized canonical* if the discrepancies of exceptional primes are at least one. Replacing the latter inequality by strict inequality gives *generalized terminal*. Throughout the theorem we impose positivity for every prime as well as the canonical bound for exceptional primes. Accordingly, exceptional coefficients of $\Delta_W$ may be zero or negative.

We use *projective* for a proper morphism admitting a relatively ample holomorphic line bundle. A $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor is relatively ample if a positive Cartier multiple is so. A proper bimeromorphic morphism is *small* if its exceptional locus contains no prime divisor. A small birational map identifies the prime divisors on its two models, and hence defines strict transforms of boundaries and canonical divisors.

<a id="stmt-thm-termination"></a>
<div id="thm:termination" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X_0$ be a normal irreducible globally Weil $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold, and let $B_0\geq0$ be a rational divisor with coefficients in $[0,1)$. Fix compatible actual canonical divisors on the birational models. Let $\mathbf M$ be the rational b-divisor represented by an analytically nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $M'$ on a projective birational morphism $X'\to X_0$ of normal compact Kähler spaces. Assume $$\begin{aligned}
 a(E;X_0,B_0+\mathbf M)&>0&&\text{for every prime over $X_0$},\\
 a(E;X_0,B_0+\mathbf M)&\geq1&&\text{for every exceptional prime}.
 \end{aligned}$$ Consider a sequence of normal globally Weil $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds $$X_0\dashrightarrow X_1\dashrightarrow X_2\dashrightarrow\cdots .$$ On $X_i$, let $B_i$ be the strict transform of $B_0$, let $M_i$ be the trace of this same $\mathbf M$, and suppose $D_i=K_{X_i}+B_i+M_i$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Suppose that every step is given by a diagram <a id="eq:step-diagram"></a>$$\label{eq:step-diagram}
 X_i\xrightarrow{\ f_i\ }Z_i
       \xleftarrow{\ f_i^+\ }X_{i+1}$$ of projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibres, where $Z_i$ is normal compact Kähler, both morphisms are nonisomorphisms, $-D_i$ is $f_i$-ample, and $D_{i+1}$ is $f_i^+$-ample. Then the sequence is finite.*

</div>

The canonical discrepancy bound and positivity are assumed only on the initial pair; both persist by the comparison proved below. The theorem imposes no pseudo-effectivity or scaling condition and no relative Picard-number restriction. It concerns finiteness of given diagrams; existence of a desired next step is a separate question.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Preceding results and the present argument

In the relative projective algebraic setting, Kawamata, Matsuda, and Matsuki proved termination for ordinary terminal $\mathbb Q$-factorial fourfolds with empty boundary. Their fourfold argument combines Shokurov’s discrepancy difficulty with a descent of surface classes <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[KMM1987](#ref-KMM1987), Theorem 5-1-15 and its proof]. Fujino extended the discrepancy and surface-count method to ordinary canonical projective fourfold pairs with effective rational boundary <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Fujino2004](#ref-Fujino2004)]. The corrected statement with $\lfloor B\rfloor=0$ is <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Fujino2005Addendum](#ref-Fujino2005Addendum), Theorem 5.1]; its flipping contractions need not have relative Picard number one. Two parts of this work directly underlie our proof. First, the target of a canonical flip is terminal near general points of its codimension-two flipped locus, which permits the blowup discrepancy calculation <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Fujino2004](#ref-Fujino2004), Lemma 2.2]. Second, the corrected low-discrepancy count makes its exclusions by centre, so that they apply to repeated blowups above a boundary surface and not only to its first blowup <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Fujino2005Addendum](#ref-Fujino2005Addendum), Proposition 3.1 and Lemma 3.2].

Generalized pairs retain nef information on a higher birational model; see the generalized polarized pairs of Birkar and Zhang <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BirkarZhang2016](#ref-BirkarZhang2016), Definition 1.4 and Section 4]. Chen and Tsakanikas proved termination of every sequence of flips for pseudo-effective four-dimensional NQC log canonical generalized pairs in the relative projective algebraic setting <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[ChenTsakanikas2023](#ref-ChenTsakanikas2023), Theorem 1.1]; see also <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Moraga2025](#ref-Moraga2025), Theorem 4.4]. Here NQC means that the nef data are finite nonnegative real combinations of nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier data. The rational nef datum used here is NQC in the algebraic setting. Their result allows log canonical singularities and real NQC data. Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a> instead concerns canonical singularities and fixed rational data, permits nonprojective compact Kähler spaces, and applies without pseudo-effectivity. The smooth-centre discrepancy formula for generalized pairs also appears in <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[ChenTsakanikas2023](#ref-ChenTsakanikas2023), Lemma 2.9].

In the Kähler setting, Das, Hacon, and Păun prove termination for ordinary dlt compact Kähler fourfold pairs whose adjoint is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective divisor, assuming the successive models remain Kähler <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DHP2024](#ref-DHP2024), Theorem 6.6]. Hacon and Xie prove termination of a minimal model program with scaling for strongly $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler generalized klt pairs whose boundary plus nef part is big, in arbitrary dimension <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[HX2026](#ref-HX2026), Theorem 1.5]. The present theorem treats every given sequence in its canonical rational setting, without either of these positivity or scaling conditions.

The ordinary terminal Kähler fourfold argument in <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[OrdinaryKahler](#ref-OrdinaryKahler), Section 4] uses spans of analytic cycle classes in place of algebraic cycle classes. We retain this organization and establish the generalized-canonical version. Its essential points are local ordinary terminality along flipped surfaces, a finite count of places below log discrepancy two allowing zero exceptional boundary coefficients, and a fixed denominator for witness discrepancies at smooth general points. The cycle comparison is proved for compact reduced analytic spaces, so it applies directly to boundary components even when they are nonnormal. The proof reproduces these arguments in full and invokes no termination theorem. Its analytic negativity input is Wang’s lemma <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Wang2021](#ref-Wang2021), Lemma 1.3 and Appendices A–B]. The references to <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[OrdinaryKahler](#ref-OrdinaryKahler)] concern only this negativity statement, terminal smoothness in codimension two, and the cycle comparison. No canonical bundle formula or absolute supporting-contraction theorem from that paper is used: the small projective diagrams are part of the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a>.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof strategy

Dimension four links the two parts of the proof. Smallness bounds both exceptional loci by dimension two, and the opposite ample signs force their dimensions to sum to at least three. Once the target locus contains no surface, the source locus must therefore contain one. A surface also has codimension two: its blowup at a smooth general point has ordinary log discrepancy two. This connects the discrepancy threshold to the surface classes that will be counted.

Surfaces contained in the source and target exceptional loci are called *flipping* and *flipped surfaces*, respectively. First we prove strict discrepancy increase for every place centred in either exceptional locus. Thus a place exceptional over the target and centred in a flipped surface has log discrepancy strictly greater than one, even when its source discrepancy equals one. Effectivity of the boundary and of the difference between the pulled-back nef trace and the nef datum then give ordinary terminality near a general point of that surface. Terminal smoothness in codimension two makes the target smooth there. This is the precise smoothness needed in the proof.

Blowing up a flipped surface at such points supplies a global prime divisor, called its *witness*. Choose one integer $m>0$ such that $mM'$ is Cartier and $mB_0$ has integral coefficients. The integral Weil divisor $mM_T$ is Cartier near the smooth general point of every flipped surface on a target $T$. The witness calculation consequently puts its target log discrepancy in $$(1,2]\cap m^{-1}\mathbb Z.$$ Its discrepancy strictly increases at each use, so each fixed place can serve as a witness at most $m$ times. This count uses no uniform bound on the Cartier indices of the nef traces elsewhere on the models.

We combine this finite set of values with a finite set of possible places, treating the positive boundary coefficients in decreasing order. If a flipped surface lies in a component of coefficient $b$, its witness has source discrepancy strictly below $2-b$. On the first model of a fixed tail, monotonicity keeps its discrepancy below $2-b$. The low-place lemma puts these divisors in a finite set, except possibly those centred on surfaces in a positive boundary component of coefficient $d$, with discrepancy at least $2-d$. Thus $d>b$. The induction has already excluded flipping surfaces in those higher-coefficient components, so these centres persist through the common isomorphic opens and cannot become flipped surfaces. At each coefficient stage the possible divisorial witnesses thus belong to a fixed finite set.

Once these witnesses are exhausted, the target exceptional locus contains no surface in the boundary components of the current coefficient. On each such component, Borel–Moore localization compares its analytic surface classes with those of the source component. Every removed source surface gives a nonzero kernel class by Kähler positivity. The rank consequently drops at each remaining step affecting such a surface. After the boundary components have been treated, the witness argument with threshold two excludes every remaining flipped surface. The same cycle comparison on the fourfolds then gives strict rank loss at every step.

Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">2</a> fixes the analytic foundations and common models. Section <a href="#sec:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparison">3</a> proves the strict one-step comparison. Section <a href="#sec:low-places" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:low-places">4</a> establishes the low-place count. Section <a href="#sec:witnesses" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:witnesses">5</a> proves generic terminality along flipped surfaces and constructs witnesses with finitely many possible discrepancy values. Section <a href="#sec:cycles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cycles">6</a> proves the cycle-rank comparison, and Section <a href="#sec:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:termination">7</a> combines these ingredients.

<a id="sec:foundations"></a>
### Analytic foundations and common models

We will compare actual divisors on common models and later prove smoothness at general points of flipped surfaces. This section records the analytic facts needed for these two purposes. All discrepancy comparisons use the fixed b-divisor $\mathbf M$ from Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a>.

<a id="subsec:places"></a>
#### Places and projective resolutions

A *place* is a global prime divisor on a normal proper bimeromorphic model, identified with its strict transforms on common higher models. Its centre is its reduced irreducible image. A place is exceptional over a model when its centre has codimension at least two. This description does not identify places solely through fields of global meromorphic functions. Properness makes the centres compact analytic subspaces. When checking a local singularity condition, we also use divisors on proper modifications of analytic neighbourhoods; we refer to these explicitly as *local places*.

Every divisor in a pullback comparison is an actual divisor, with the compatible canonical representatives prescribed in Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a>. Ordinary relative canonical coefficients can be computed from local pluricanonical equations after clearing a Cartier multiple; over a smooth space they are the orders of the Jacobian. These computations localize. Indeed, a ratio of representatives that is a unit off a codimension-two subset of a normal base extends as a unit across that subset. Thus agreement on the isomorphic opens gives the same local discrepancy coefficients.

For a proper bimeromorphic morphism $r:V\to T$ of normal spaces, the set of points with positive-dimensional fibres is closed analytic. Off this subset the morphism is finite and bimeromorphic, hence an isomorphism by normality. The subset has codimension at least two: its inverse image is a proper analytic subset of $V$, and the fibre-dimension theorem excludes a codimension-one image with positive-dimensional fibres. Fibres are connected by Stein factorization. A point where the morphism is a local isomorphism is isolated in its fibre, so it cannot belong to a connected positive-dimensional fibre. The exceptional locus is therefore exactly the inverse image of this subset.

We use normalization, proper mapping, fibre dimension, Stein factorization, and resolution and principalization in the complex analytic category. The last two permit simultaneous resolution of finitely many divisors and coherent ideals by projective modifications; see <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[BierstoneMilman1997](#ref-BierstoneMilman1997), Theorems 1.6, 1.10, and 13.2(1)]. Projective morphisms are preserved under base change and restriction to closed analytic subspaces; finite morphisms are projective. The projective morphisms used here can also be composed: a sufficiently large twist of one relative ample line bundle by the pullback of the next is relatively ample for the composite, with compactness giving a uniform choice.

<a id="stmt-prop-common-models"></a>
<div id="prop:common-models" class="proposition">

**Proposition 2** (Common models). *Any finite portion of the sequence in Theorem <a href="#thm:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:termination">1</a> has a smooth common model dominating its members and $X'$, projective over each member. It may also dominate any prescribed projective modification of one of those members. For a chosen member $T$, it can be arranged that the exceptional locus over $T$ is divisorial and that its union with the strict transform of $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}B_T$ has simple normal crossings. The model carries $\mathbf M$ as the pullback of $M'$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-common-models"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-common-models</summary>

*Proof.* Enlarge the finite portion to an initial segment starting at $X_0$. For one step, take the reduced component of $X_i\times_{Z_i}X_{i+1}$ containing the graph over the common isomorphic open. It is projective over both models, and its normalization remains so. Combine these graphs and $X'\to X_0$ by successive dominating components of fibre products, then normalize and resolve. To include a prescribed projective modification of a member, take one further fibre product over that member before resolving. Base change, restriction, finite normalization, and composition show that all resulting morphisms to the members of the segment are projective.

Fix $T$. Choose a closed analytic subset of codimension at least two outside which the map is an isomorphism, $T$ is smooth, and the boundary components are smooth and disjoint; include their singular loci and pairwise intersections in this subset. Principalize the pullback of its ideal and resolve the total divisor without changing this open set. The inverse image of the chosen subset is now a divisor whose components are exceptional over $T$; it contains the entire exceptional locus. Resolving together with the strict boundary gives the claimed normal-crossing condition. Pulling back $M'$ preserves the fixed data. ◻

</details>

A further common resolution with a model carrying any prescribed place allows comparison of its discrepancy. That carrying model need not itself be projective, and the further resolution is only required to be proper. Projectivity of the models in Proposition <a href="#prop:common-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:common-models">2</a> will be used when applying negativity and when constructing divisorial witnesses.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Negativity for actual divisors

For a projective morphism, relative nefness of a rational divisor in the following lemma is the degree condition on all contracted curves. This is distinct from absolute analytic nefness.

<a id="stmt-lem-negativity"></a>
<div id="lem:negativity" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Analytic negativity). *Let $r:V\to T$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism of normal complex spaces and let $F$ be a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $V$. If $-F$ has nonnegative degree on every contracted curve and $r_*F$ is effective, then $F$ is effective.*

</div>

This is <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Wang2021](#ref-Wang2021), Lemma 1.3 and Appendix A], after clearing a Cartier multiple. The relative degree criterion in the projective case is explicit in <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Wang2021](#ref-Wang2021), Appendix B]. Effectivity is that of the actual divisor, not merely its class. The corresponding statement is <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[OrdinaryKahler](#ref-OrdinaryKahler), Lemma 2.3(i)]. We apply it only when $r_*F=0$.

An effective $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on a normal space pulls back effectively: after multiplying, its local meromorphic equation has no divisorial poles, hence is holomorphic by normality. It also has nonnegative degree on every compact curve not contained in its support, by restriction to the normalization of that curve. We will use both facts.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Ordinary terminal singularities in codimension two

The following local statement will be applied on an open set meeting a flipped surface. We include its surface proof, as in <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[OrdinaryKahler](#ref-OrdinaryKahler), Lemma 4.4]. Ordinary terminality here means that every local exceptional place has ordinary log discrepancy strictly greater than one.

<a id="stmt-lem-terminal-smooth"></a>
<div id="lem:terminal-smooth" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *A normal complex fourfold with a canonical rational line bundle and ordinary terminal singularities is smooth in codimension two.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-terminal-smooth"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-terminal-smooth</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose the singular locus has a codimension-two component. Near its general smooth point, choose a holomorphic projection to $\mathbb C^2$ submersive on that component. Take a log resolution which is an isomorphism over the regular locus. Choose a general fibre $S$ of the projection: its inverse image $\widetilde S$ is smooth, misses exceptional strata whose images have dimension less than two, and meets all remaining strata transversely. These choices follow by generic smoothness on the resolution and its finitely many strata. Write $\rho:\widetilde S\to S$ for the induced resolution. Terminal singularities are klt, so the ambient space has rational singularities by <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[FujinoAnalytic2022](#ref-FujinoAnalytic2022), Theorem 3.12], and is Cohen–Macaulay <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Greb2011](#ref-Greb2011), Remark 2.1]. The fibre has the expected codimension two. Its two defining parameters therefore form a regular sequence, so $S$ is Cohen–Macaulay. It is regular away from isolated points by the general choice of fibre. Serre’s criterion makes $S$ normal.

For the regular sequence, the Koszul calculation of the dualizing sheaf gives $\omega_S\simeq\omega_T\otimes\mathcal O_S$, where $T$ denotes the ambient neighbourhood; the two slice equations trivialize the normal determinant. This uses the local analytic Ext description of the dualizing sheaf <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[RSW2017](#ref-RSW2017), pp. 3–4]. Away from the isolated intersections with the ambient singular locus, a local Cartier pluricanonical power restricts accordingly, and the identification extends reflexively over the normal surface. Applying adjunction also on the smooth resolution therefore restricts its relative canonical equation to $$A:=K_{\widetilde S}-\rho^*K_S=\sum_j a_jE_j,
 \qquad a_j>0.$$ Here each exceptional curve is a transverse restriction of an exceptional divisor on the resolution; terminality gives its positive coefficient.
